Note that we include the normalization factors in the definition of the tangential vectors, since in contrast to the normal vector, this factor does not cancel out with the Jacobian
\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{\Gamma}^{\omega}
when transforming to the fixed domain.
Moreover, we set

\widehat{\boldsymbol{n}}^{\eta^{\delta}}=\Bigl(-\partial_{\widehat{x}}\Bigl(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\delta}\Big|_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\Bigr),-\partial_{\widehat{y}}\Bigl(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\delta}\Big|_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\Bigr),1\Bigr).

The weak formulation of the regularized FPSI problem, involving a
fixed fluid domain
, now reads:

Definition 4.6

(Weak formulation of the regularized problem with fixed fluid domain)
.

A tuple
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}},\widehat{\omega},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{p})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{sol}}^{\omega}
is a weak solution to the regularized 3D nonlinearly coupled FPSI problem with regularization parameter
\delta
if for any test function
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}},\widehat{\varphi},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}},\widehat{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{test}}^{\omega}
, the following weak formulation holds:

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widehat{\omega}}{R}\Bigr)\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widehat{\omega}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl[\bigl(((\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{w}})\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega})\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\bigr)\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}

\displaystyle-\bigl(((\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{w}})\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega})\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\bigr)\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\bigr]\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t-\frac{1}{2R}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathrm{f}}}(\partial_{t}\widehat{\omega})\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{n}}^{\omega}-\widehat{\zeta}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{n}}^{\omega})\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}\,\mathrm{d}t+2\nu\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widehat{\omega}}{R}\Bigr)\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}):\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}})\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}-\widehat{p}\Bigr)(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}})\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{n}}^{\omega}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}\,\mathrm{d}t+\beta\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{\Gamma}^{\omega}(\widehat{\zeta}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}})\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{i}^{\omega}(\boldsymbol{\psi}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}})\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{i}^{\omega}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\partial_{t}\widehat{\omega}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\widehat{\Delta}\widehat{\omega}\cdot\widehat{\Delta}\widehat{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}\,\mathrm{d}t-\rho_{\int}{}_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle+2\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}):\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t+\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}(\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}})(\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t}}

\displaystyle+2\mu_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}):\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t+\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}(\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}})(\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t-\alpha\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}\widehat{p}\widehat{\nabla}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t}}}

\displaystyle-c_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{p}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{r}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t-\alpha\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}\widehat{r}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t-\alpha\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}(\widehat{\zeta}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{n}}^{\eta^{\delta}})\widehat{r}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}\,\mathrm{d}t}}

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}\widehat{\nabla}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}\widehat{p}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}\widehat{r}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}((\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}}-\widehat{\zeta}\boldsymbol{e}_{3})\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{n}}^{\omega})\widehat{r}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}\,\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(0)}\boldsymbol{u}(0)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}(0)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\partial_{t}\widehat{\omega}(0)\cdot\widehat{\varphi}(0)\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}+\rho_{\int}{}_{\widehat{\Omega}\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(0)\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(0)\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+c_{0}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{p}(0)\cdot\widehat{r}(0)\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},}}

where we recall the definitions of
\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{J}}_{\Gamma}^{\omega}
from (
3.3
),
\widehat{\boldsymbol{w}}
from (
3.6
),
\widehat{\nabla}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega}
from (
3.5
),
\widehat{\nabla}_{^{\eta^{\delta}}}
from (
3.7
) as well as
\widehat{\zeta}
from (
3.9
).

4.2.
A formal energy inequality

Here we show that the weak formulation of the regularized FPSI problem is ``energy consistent'' with the original, non-regularized problem in the sense that the two problems satisfy the same type of energy estimate.
The only difference is that the integrals over the moving Biot domain
\Omega_{(}t)
in the energy equality of the non-regularized problem (
3.10
) are now given over the regularized moving Biot domain
\Omega_{^{\delta}}(t)
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose that a weak solution to the regularized FPSI problem given in
Subsection 4.1
exists and is sufficiently smooth. Then the following energy equality holds:

E^{K}(T)+E^{E}(T)+\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(D_{\mathrm{f}}^{V}(t)+D_{^{V}}(t)+D_{\mathrm{f}^{V}(t)+D_{\beta}^{V}(t)\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}t=E^{K}(0)+E^{E}(0),}

where

E^{K}(t)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(t)}|\boldsymbol{u}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{p}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}|\partial_{t}\widehat{\omega}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}}}

is the total kinetic energy of the problem,

E^{E}(t)=\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}})(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}|\widehat{\Delta}\widehat{\omega}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}}}

is the elastic energy of the Biot poroelastic matrix and the reticular plate, and

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{f}}^{V}(t)

\displaystyle=2\nu\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(t)}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{u})(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x},\kern 5.0ptD_{^{V}}(t)=2\mu_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}})(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}},}}

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{f}^{V}(t)}

\displaystyle=\kappa\int_{\Omega_{^{\delta}}(t)}|\nabla p(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0ptD_{\beta}^{V}(t)=\beta\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Gamma(t)}|(\boldsymbol{\xi}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}(t))\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S,

are the dissipation terms due to the fluid viscosity
D_{\mathrm{f}}^{V}
, the viscosity of the Biot poro(visco)elastic matrix
D_{^{V}}
, permeability effects
D_{\mathrm{f}^{V}}
, and friction in the Beavers–Joseph–Saffman slip condition
D_{\beta}^{V}
.

The proof of this (formal) energy equality is based on using the test functions
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{v}},\widehat{\varphi},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\psi}},\widehat{r})=(\widehat{\boldsymbol{u}},\widehat{\omega},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{p})
, switching between the formulations on the fixed and moving domains and canceling appropriate terms. For more details one can also see the proof of Lemma 5.1 in
[
30
]
.

4.3.
Main result on the existence of a weak solution to the regularized problem

The main result of this work is the proof of the existence of a weak solution to the regularized nonlinearly coupled FPSI problem in 3D, made precise in
Subsection 4.1
.

Theorem 4.8
.

Suppose that the problem parameters satisfy
\rho
,
\mu_{\mathrm{e}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}
,
\alpha
,
\rho_{\mathrm{p}}
,
\nu>0
, and assume that the viscosity parameters of the Biot layer fulfill
\mu_{\mathrm{v}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}\geq 0
.
Moreover, suppose that the initial data satisfy
\widehat{\omega}_{0}\in\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\widehat{\Gamma})
for the plate displacement,
\widehat{\zeta}_{0}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\widehat{\Gamma})
for the plate velocity,
\widehat{p}_{0}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{)}
for the Biot pore pressure,
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(0))^{3}
for the fluid velocity with the additional property that
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
is divergence free,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\widehat{\Omega}_{)}^{3}
for the Biot displacement and

(1)

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{0}\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\widehat{\Omega}_{)}^{3}
for the Biot velocity in the viscoelastic case
\mu_{\mathrm{v}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}>0
, and

(2)

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{0}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\widehat{\Omega}_{)}^{3}
for the Biot velocity in the purely elastic case
\mu_{\mathrm{v}}=\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}=0
.

Furthermore, assume that there is
R_{0}\in(0,R)
with
|\widehat{\omega}_{0}|\leq R_{0}
, the compatibility conditions
\left.\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}\right|_{\widehat{\Gamma}}=\widehat{\omega}_{0}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}
as well as
\left.\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{0}\right|_{\widehat{\Gamma}}=\widehat{\zeta}_{0}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}
are satisfied, and that for some arbitrary but fixed regularization parameter
\delta>0
, we have that
\det(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}^{\delta})>0
.

Then
there exists
T>0
such that the regularized 3D nonlinearly coupled FPSI problem, made precise in
Subsection 4.1
,
admits a weak solution

(\boldsymbol{u},\widehat{\omega},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\widehat{p})
on
[0,T]
in the sense of
Subsection 4.1
.

Moreover, the weak solution satisfies the following
energy estimate
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(t)}\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\bigl|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(t)\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}\bigl|\widehat{p}(t)\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}})(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}}}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}\bigl|\widehat{\zeta}(t)\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\widehat{\Gamma}}|\widehat{\Delta}\widehat{\omega}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}+2\nu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f},N}(s)}\bigl|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{u})(s)\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\,\mathrm{d}s+2\mu_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\boldsymbol{D}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}})(s)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle+\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\widehat{\Omega}|\widehat{\nabla}\cdot\widehat{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(s)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{\boldsymbol{x}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega^{\delta}(s)}\bigl|\nabla p(s)\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\,\mathrm{d}s+\beta\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma(s)}\bigl|(\zeta\boldsymbol{e}_{3}-\boldsymbol{u})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S\,\mathrm{d}t\leq E_{0}.}

The remainder of this paper is devoted to proving
Theorem 4.8
. The proof will be based on a splitting approach, which was first introduced to study weak solutions to moving boundary problems in
[
41
]
.
For notational simplicity, we omit below the
\widehat{}
notation distinguishing quantities defined on the reference domain from those on the physical domain, including the domains themselves, as the distinction between the two will be clear from the context.

5.
The splitting scheme

Similar to the strategies employed in
[
41
,
30
]
, the general approach is to semidiscretize the problem in time by introducing time steps
\Delta t=\nicefrac{{T}}{{N}}
and partitioning the interval
[0,T]
into
N
subintervals of length
\Delta t
.
On each subinterval, we consider two subproblems:

(1)

a plate subproblem, which governs the elastodynamics of the reticular plate and is used to update the plate displacement and velocity; and

(2)

a fluid/Biot subproblem, which updates the fluid velocity, Biot displacement, Biot pore pressure, and plate velocity.

The solution from the first subproblem is used as input data for the second.

The approximations of the fluid velocity, plate displacement and velocity, and Biot poro(visco)elastic material displacement and pressure, will be denoted by

(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}},\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}},\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}},p_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}),\kern 5.0pt\text{for}\kern 5.0ptn=0,1,\dots,N\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pti=0,1.

They are defined on the given time subinterval
[n\Delta t,(n+1)\Delta t]
.
Moreover, the quantities with superscript
n+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
indicate the approximate solutions after solving the plate subproblem, while the quantities with superscript
n+1
represent the approximate solutions after solving the fluid/Biot subproblem.

We remark that we will work on the weak formulation given in terms of the fixed reference domain, defined in
Subsection 4.1
.
Consequently, we will semi-discretize the regularized weak formulation on the fixed reference domain.
Concerning the approximation of time derivatives, we will use backward Euler definition and employ the notation

(5.1)

\dot{f}_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}=\frac{f_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}-f_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}-1}}{\Delta t}.

Let us remark at this stage that due to the backward Euler discretization as described in (
5.1
), there can be negative subscripts.
We will explicitly define the quantities
f_{-1}
and
f_{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
when necessary.

5.1.
The plate subproblem

In this step, only the plate quantities
\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
and
\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
are updated, while the fluid and Biot quantities remain unchanged:

\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n},\kern 5.0pt\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0ptp_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=p_{N}^{n}.

The plate displacement
\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
and velocity
\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
are determined by using the weak formulation of the plate subproblems, i.e., find
\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\in\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)
and
\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\in\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)

such that

(5.2)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\biggl(\frac{\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Delta t}\biggr)\cdot\phi\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\phi\,\mathrm{d}S,

\displaystyle\text{for all}\kern 5.0pt\phi\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma),

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{\Gamma}\biggl(\frac{\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\zeta_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\biggr)\cdot\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S+\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\Delta\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\text{for all}\kern 5.0pt\varphi\in\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma).

For the first subinterval, i.e., for
n=0
, we set
\omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\omega(0)
and
\zeta_{N}^{0}=\zeta(0)
, and these quantities satisfy
\omega(0)\boldsymbol{e}_{3}=\left.\boldsymbol{\eta}(0)\right|_{\Gamma}
as well as
\zeta(0)\boldsymbol{e}_{3}=\boldsymbol{\xi}(0)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists a unique solution
\bigl(\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)
to the semidiscretized plate subproblem (
5.2
), and
\bigl(\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}},\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)
satisfies the energy equality

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\zeta_{N}^{n}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\Delta\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\Delta(\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\zeta_{N}^{n}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\Delta\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S.

The proof is a consequence of Lax–Milgram lemma, similar to the proof of Lemma 6.1 in
[
30
]
.

5.2.
The fluid and Biot subproblem

In this subproblem the fluid and Biot quantities are updated.
However, by the kinematic coupling condition (
2.10
) involving the Biot medium and plate displacement, it is necessary to update the plate velocity as well.
Thus, only the plate displacement stays intact, i.e.,
\omega_{N}^{n+1}=\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
, while the other quantities are recovered from the corresponding weak formulation.

For the weak formulation, we define the solution and test space by

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigl\{(\boldsymbol{u},\zeta,\boldsymbol{\eta},p)\in V_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\times\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)\times V_{\mathrm{d}}\times V_{\mathrm{p}}\bigr\}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{N}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{(\boldsymbol{v},\varphi,\boldsymbol{\psi},r)\in V_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\times\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)\times V_{\mathrm{d}}\times V_{\mathrm{p}}:\boldsymbol{\psi}=\varphi\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\kern 5.0pt\text{on}\kern 5.0pt\Gamma\},

respectively, where
V_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}
,
V_{\mathrm{d}}
and
V_{\mathrm{p}}
are given in (
4.2
), (
4.3
)
1
and (
4.4
)
1
.

The weak formulation of the present fluid and Biot subproblem is now given as follows.

Definition 5.2
.

We say that the fluid and Biot subproblem in the splitting scheme admits a weak solution if there are
(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1},\zeta_{N}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1},p_{N}^{n+1})\in\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n+1}
such that for all test functions
(\boldsymbol{v},\varphi,\boldsymbol{\psi},r)\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}^{n+1}
defined on the (fixed) reference domain, the following holds:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+2\nu\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}):\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{v})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}-p_{N}^{n+1}\Bigr)(\boldsymbol{\psi}-\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\Bigl[\Bigl(\Bigl(\Bigl(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}-\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\frac{R+z}{R}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\Bigr)\cdot\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}

\displaystyle-\Bigl(\Bigl(\Bigl(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}-\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\frac{R+z}{R}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\Bigr)\cdot\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\Bigr]\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{1}{2R}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{v})\,\mathrm{d}S+\beta\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\mathcal{J}_{\Gamma}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{\psi}-\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle+\rho_{\int}{}_{{\Omega}\Bigl(\frac{\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S+2\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}

\displaystyle+\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+2\mu_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{{\Omega}\boldsymbol{D}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{{\Omega}(\nabla\cdot\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle-\alpha\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+c_{0}\int_{{\Omega}\frac{p_{N}^{n+1}-p_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}r\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}-\alpha\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}r\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle-\alpha\int_{\Gamma}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}})r\,\mathrm{d}S+\kappa\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}r\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}-\int_{\Gamma}((\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{N}^{n}})r\,\mathrm{d}S=0}

as well as
\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}S=\int_{\Gamma}\zeta_{N}^{n+1}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\cdot\boldsymbol{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}S
for all
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)^{3}
.

Note that in the case
n=0
, a negative subscript appears in the definition of
\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{0}
in the Euler discretization.
Thus, we set
\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{0}
to be the initial plate velocity
\zeta_{0}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}
.

Below, we state the existence result of a unique weak solution to the fluid and Biot subproblem as discussed in
Subsection 5.2
.

Lemma 5.3
.

The semidiscretized fluid and Biot subproblem in
Subsection 5.2
admits a unique weak solution in the sense of
Subsection 5.2
under the following assumptions:

(1)

Assumption 1.A (Boundedness of plate displacement away from
R
):

There is a positive constant
R_{0}
so that
|\omega_{N}^{k+\frac{i}{2}}|\leq R_{0}<R
for all
k=0,1,\dots,n
and
i=0,1
.

(2)

Assumption 2.A (Invertibility of
\Id+(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}
):

The map
\Id+(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}\colon\Omega_{\to}(\Omega_{)}{}_{N}^{n,\delta}
,
which maps the fixed Biot domain
\Omega
onto
(\Omega_{)}{}_{N}^{n,\delta}
, is invertible.

(3)

Assumption 3.A (Positivity of
\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}=\det(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta})
):
There exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that for all
N
, we have
\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}=\det\bigl(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}\bigr)\geq c_{0}>0
for all
n=0,1,\dots,N
.

Moreover, the resulting weak solution to the fluid and Biot subproblem satisfies the following energy equality:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n+1}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle+2\nu(\Delta t)\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+2\mu_{\mathrm{v}}(\Delta t)\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}(\Delta t)\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}

\displaystyle+\kappa(\Delta t)\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\bigl|\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\beta(\Delta t)\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\mathcal{J}_{\Gamma}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigl|(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|p_{N}^{n+1}-p_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|p_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}

\displaystyle+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}.}}

The proof of
Subsection 5.2
follows from the Lax–Milgram lemma after addressing two main challenges. The first is the challenge associated with the fact that
the bilinear form corresponding to the weak formulation is not coercive on
V_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\times V_{\mathrm{d}}\times V_{\mathrm{p}}
due to a mismatch in hyperbolic-parabolic scaling.
This can be remedied by rescaling the test functions as follows:

\boldsymbol{v}\mapsto(\Delta t)\boldsymbol{v}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0ptr\mapsto(\Delta t)r.

We note that the rescaled test functions are admissible as they satisfy
((\Delta t)^{-1}\boldsymbol{v},\varphi,\boldsymbol{\psi},(\Delta t)^{-1}r)\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}^{n+1}
.

The second challenge is associated with the fact that the validity of Korn's inequality for the Biot domain cannot be guaranteed a priori.
However, an explicit calculation reveals the following Korn inequality.

Lemma 5.4

(Korn's inequality for
\Omega
)
.

For
\boldsymbol{\eta}\in V_{\mathrm{d}}
, it holds that
\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}
.

The proof is analogous to the one of
[
30
, Prop. 6.1]
, so we omit the details.

The rest of the proof of
Subsection 5.2
follows the same calculations as the proof of Lemma 6.2 in
[
30
]
.

Our goal is to prove that the sequences of approximate solutions defined by this splitting scheme, converge to a solution of the regularized, nonlinearly coupled FPSI problem, as the time step
\Delta t
tends to zero. The first step in this direction is to obtain uniform estimate on approximate solution. For this purpose it is useful to present the semidiscretized scheme in monolithic form, and derive the appropriate energy estimates. We present those estimates in the next section.

5.3.
Weak formulation and energy equality for the coupled semidiscrete problem

We derive the monolithic (coupled) formulation of the semidiscrete problem by combining the weak formulation of the semidiscretized plate subproblem (
5.2
) with the weak formulation of the semidiscretized fluid and Biot subproblem in
Subsection 5.2
, and using the fact that

\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\zeta_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\cdot\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S+\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\cdot\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S=\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\cdot\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S.

The following monolithic weak formulation holds:

Definition 5.5
.

For each fixed
n\in\{1,...,N\}
, the weak formulation of the semidiscretized problem above, written in monolithic form, reads: the functions
(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1},\zeta_{N}^{n+1},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1},p_{N}^{n+1})\in\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n+1}
and
\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
,
\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\in\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)
are a weak solution if for all test functions
(\boldsymbol{v},\varphi,\boldsymbol{\psi},r)\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}^{n+1}
,
\phi\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)
and
\boldsymbol{\phi}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)^{3}
, they satisfy the following weak formulation:

(5.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\dot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+2\nu\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}):\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{v})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}-p_{N}^{n+1}\Bigr)(\boldsymbol{\psi}-\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\Bigl[\Bigl(\Bigl(\Bigl(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}-\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\frac{R+z}{R}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\Bigr)\cdot\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}

\displaystyle-\Bigl(\Bigl(\Bigl(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}-\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\frac{R+z}{R}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\Bigr)\cdot\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\Bigr)\boldsymbol{v}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\Bigr]\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{1}{2R}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{v})\,\mathrm{d}S+\beta\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\mathcal{J}_{\Gamma}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{\psi}-\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\,\mathrm{d}S

\displaystyle+\rho_{\int}{}_{{\Omega}\Bigl(\frac{\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\rho_{\mathrm{p}}\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S+2\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}

\displaystyle+\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+2\mu_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{{\Omega}\boldsymbol{D}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}):\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}\int_{{\Omega}(\nabla\cdot\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle-\alpha\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+c_{0}\int_{{\Omega}\frac{p_{N}^{n+1}-p_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}r\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}-\alpha\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}r\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle-\alpha\int_{\Gamma}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}})r\,\mathrm{d}S+\kappa\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}r\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\bigl((\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigr)r\,\mathrm{d}S+\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\Delta\varphi\,\mathrm{d}S=0\kern 5.0pt\text{as well as}\kern 5.0pt

(5.5)

\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\phi\,\mathrm{d}S=\int_{\Gamma}\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\phi\,\mathrm{d}S\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt\int_{\Gamma}\Bigl(\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\Bigr)\cdot\boldsymbol{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}S=\int_{\Gamma}\zeta_{N}^{n+1}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\cdot\boldsymbol{\phi}\,\mathrm{d}S.

By recalling that
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}
,
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}
,
p_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=p_{N}^{n}
and
\omega_{N}^{n+1}=\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}
, and introducing the following notation for discrete total energy
E_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}
and discrete dissipation
D_{N}^{n+1}
for
i=1,2
:

\displaystyle E_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|p_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\Delta\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S,}}}

\displaystyle D_{N}^{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2\nu(\Delta t)\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+2\mu_{\mathrm{v}}(\Delta t)\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\lambda_{\mathrm{v}}(\Delta t)\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}

\displaystyle+\kappa(\Delta t)\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\bigl|\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\beta(\Delta t)\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Gamma}\mathcal{J}_{\Gamma}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigl|(\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S,}

we can derive the following energy estimate:

Lemma 5.6
.

A weak solution to the semidiscretized coupled problem in
Subsection 5.3
satisfies

E_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\Delta(\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S=E_{N}^{n}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt

\displaystyle E_{N}^{n+1}+D_{N}^{n+1}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|p_{N}^{n+1}-p_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=E_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}.}}}

Remark 5.7
.

The terms in the energy equality above that are not included in the definitions of the total energy
E_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}
and dissipation
D_{N}^{n+1}
, are due to numerical dissipation.

This energy estimate serves as the cornerstone for the uniform bounds established in the next section, as it implies that both
E_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}
and
\sum_{n=1}^{N}D_{N}^{n}
are uniformly bounded by a constant
C
independent of
n
and
N
. Recall that
E_{N}^{n+\frac{i}{2}}
and
D_{N}^{n}
represent the
time-discrete
total energy and dissipation, respectively. However, our ultimate goal is to obtain uniform estimates for the approximate
solutions
themselves. Approximate solutions are functions of both space and time, not only space, given at discrete time points.
To that end, we introduce the corresponding approximate solutions in the next section, and derive uniform boundedness estimates and weak convergence results that will be useful when passing to the limit, as the time-discretization parameter tends to zero.

6.
Approximate solutions: uniform boundedness and weak convergence results

In this section, we extend the approximations defined at a discrete number of time point via the splitting scheme presented in
Section 5
,
to approximate solutions defined on the entire time interval
(0,T)
.

First, for the fluid velocity
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
, the Biot displacement
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}
, the pore pressure
p_{N}
, the plate displacement
\omega_{N}
as well as the plate velocity
\zeta_{N}
, we introduce piecewise constant approximations: for
t\in\bigl((n-1)\Delta t,n\Delta t\bigr]
, we set

(6.1)

\boldsymbol{u}_{N}(t)=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n},\kern 5.0pt\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}(t)=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n},\kern 5.0ptp_{N}(t)=p_{N}^{n},\kern 5.0pt\omega_{N}(t)=\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\kern 5.0pt\zeta_{N}(t)=\zeta_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt\zeta_{N}^{*}(t)=\zeta_{N}^{n}.

These piecewise constant approximations have a disadvantage that they possibly exhibit jump discontinuities.
Thus, in order to pave the way for higher regularity in time, and for the limit passage in
Section 8
, we also employ linear interpolations for the fluid velocity
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
, for the Biot displacement
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}
, the pore pressure
p_{N}

and the plate displacement
\omega_{N}
, i.e., we introduce

\overline{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}(n\Delta t)=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n},\kern 5.0pt\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}(n\Delta t)=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n},\kern 5.0pt\overline{p}_{N}(n\Delta t)=p_{N}^{n}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt\overline{\omega}_{N}(n\Delta t)=\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}},\kern 5.0pt\text{for}\kern 5.0ptn=0,1,\dots,N.

In the above, we formally set
\omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\omega_{0}
.
By construction of the splitting scheme, see also (
5.5
), for
t\in(n\Delta t,(n+1)\Delta t]
, we observe that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Delta t}=\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=\zeta_{N}(t),\kern 5.0pt\partial_{t}\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}(t)=\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}=\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt

\displaystyle\left.\partial_{t}\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}(t)\right|_{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}}{\Delta t}\right|_{\Gamma}=\zeta_{N}^{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}=\zeta_{N}^{*}(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{3}.

Similar relations are also valid for the fluid velocity
\overline{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}
as well as the fluid pore pressure
\overline{p}_{N}
.

In order to derive uniform boundedness of approximate solutions, we make the following a priori assumptions, which will be later justified by showing that they indeed hold on a sufficiently small time interval
[0,T]
for
T>0
, as shown in
Section 6
below:

A priori assumptions on uniform boundedness of geometric quantities:

(1)

Assumption 1.B (Uniform boundedness of displacements):

There is a constant
R_{\max}>0
so that for all
N
, we have
|\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}|\leq R_{\max}<R
, for all
n=0,1,\dots,N
, and
\left|\left.(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}\right|_{\Gamma}\right|\leq R_{\max}<R
for all
n=0,1,\dots,N
.

(2)

Assumption 2.B (Uniform invertibility of the Lagrangian map):

There exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that for all
N
, we have
\det\bigl(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}\bigr)\geq c_{0}>0
for all
n=0,1,\dots,N
.

(3)

Assumption 2.C (Uniform boundedness of the Lagrangian map):

There are constants
c_{1}
,
c_{2}>0
such that for all
N
, we obtain
|(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta})^{-1}|\leq c_{1}
and
|\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}|\leq c_{2}
, for all
n=0,1,\dots,N
.

Lemma 6.1
.

(Uniform boundedness)

Suppose that Assumptions 1.B, 2.B, and 2.C hold.
Then, for all
N>0
, the approximate functions satisfy the following uniform boundedness properties:

•

the fluid velocity
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3})
and
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{1,p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3})
, for all
p\in(1,2)
,

•

the Biot displacement
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
,

•

the pore pressure
p_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)})
, and

•

the plate displacement
\omega_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma))
.

Moreover, linear interpolations satisfy the following uniform boundedness properties:

•

the Biot displacement
\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
, and

•

the plate displacement
\overline{\omega}_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma))
.

Before proving
Section 6
, we first remark that because we are working in 3D, the fluid velocity is uniformly bounded only in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{W}^{1,p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}))
, for
p\in(1,2)
, and not in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}))
, which was the case for the 2D problem.
This has important implications for the compactness arguments and the limit passage below.

Secondly, we remark that the assumptions in
Section 6
imply the two assumptions in
Subsection 5.2
. This is addressed more precisely in the following.

Remark 6.2
.

The assumptions in
Section 6
imply the two assumptions in
Subsection 5.2
:

(1)

Assumption 1.B in
Section 6
implies Assumption 1.A in
Subsection 5.2
.

(2)

Assumptions 1.B and 2.B in
Section 6
imply Assumption 2.A in
Subsection 5.2
.

Indeed, the following result from
[
19
, Theorem 5.5-2]
will help us prove this statement by providing a criterion for injectivity of a given map in terms of the existence of another injective map that coincides with the given map on the boundary:

Lemma 6.3
.

Consider a bounded Lipschitz domain
\Omega
, and assume that
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\colon\overline{\Omega}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is continuous as a map from
\overline{\Omega}
to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\mathrm{C}^{1}
as a map from
\Omega
to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and fulfills the pointwise condition
\det(\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi})>0
.
If there is an injective continuous map
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}\colon\overline{\Omega}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\left.\boldsymbol{\Phi}\right|_{\partial\Omega}=\left.\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}\right|_{\partial\Omega}
, then
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\colon\overline{\Omega}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an injective homeomorphism with
\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\overline{\Omega})=\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}(\overline{\Omega})
, and
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an injective diffeomorphism with
\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\Omega)=\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}(\Omega)
.

Indeed, Assumption 2.B implies that
\det(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta})>0
, which is one of the requirements in
item 2
. Furthermore,
\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}
possesses all the necessary regularity properties from
item 2
. Therefore, by
item 2
, it suffices to construct an injective mapping
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}\colon\overline{\Omega}_{\to}\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that
\Id+(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}=\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}
on
\partial\Omega
.

For this, we can use a standard ALE mapping, e.g.,
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}(x,y,z)=(x,y,z+(1-\frac{z}{R})(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}(x,y,0))
, which is injective by Assumption 1.B. Therefore, Assumption 2.A in
Subsection 5.2
is satisfied.
As a result of
item 2
, we also get that
(\Id+(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta})(\overline{\Omega}_{)}=\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{0}(\overline{\Omega}_{)}
.
In other words, the deformed configuration is fully determined by the behavior on the boundary.

(3)

Assumption 3.A in
Subsection 5.2
corresponds precisely to Assumption 2.B in
Section 6
.

Next, we prove
Section 6
.

Proof of
Section 6
.

The main ingredients here are the uniform energy estimates obtained in
Subsection 5.3
.
As a preparation, upon recalling the conventions from
Section 5
, we define the initial energy
E_{0}
by

\displaystyle E_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{0}}{R}\Bigr)|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\zeta_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|p_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}}}

\displaystyle+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\Delta\omega_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S,}}

indicating that
E_{0}
is independent of the discretization parameter
N
.
Next, concatenating the first part of the assertion of
Subsection 5.3
with the second one, for
E_{N}^{n}
,
E_{N}^{n+1}
as well as
D_{N}^{n+1}
, we find that

\displaystyle E_{N}^{n+1}+D_{N}^{n+1}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|p_{N}^{n+1}-p_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\Delta(\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=E_{N}^{n}.}}

Summing from
0
to
n
, and using the cancellations, we first find that

(6.3)

E_{N}^{n+1}+\sum_{j=0}^{n}D_{N}^{j+1}\leq E_{0}

for all
n=0,1,\dots,N-1
.
Similarly, we obtain that

(6.4)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\biggl(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}-\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+1}-\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{p}}}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\bigl|\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\zeta_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|p_{N}^{n+1}-p_{N}^{n}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}|\Delta(\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}S}

\displaystyle+\mu_{\mathrm{e}}\int_{{\Omega}|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{\lambda_{\mathrm{e}}}{2}\int_{{\Omega}|\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\biggr)\leq E_{0}.}}

To prove uniform boundedness of the fluid velocity we recall Assumption 1.B, which implies the existence of a constant
\tilde{C}>0
such that for all
n=0,1,\dots,N-1
and all
N
, we have
1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\geq 1-\frac{R_{\max}}{R}\geq\tilde{C}
.
Let now
t\in(0,T)
be arbitrary.
Then there is
n\in\{0,1,\dots,N-1\}
with
t\in(n\Delta t,(n+1)\Delta t]
, so it follows that
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}(t)=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}
.
Making use of the preceding uniform lower bound of
1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}
, recalling the definition of
E_{N}^{n+1}
and employing (
6.3
), we find that

(6.5)

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})}^{2}=\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\cdot\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl(1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\Bigr)\bigl|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq CE_{0},

where
C>0
is independent of
N
.
This shows the uniform boundedness of
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3})
.

The uniform boundedness with higher spatial regularity is not so straightforward:
The uniform energy estimates from
Subsection 5.3
only provide information about the
transformed symmetric part of the gradient
.
To deduce properties of the gradient, we would like to employ Korn's inequality, which now needs to be applied to the moving domain case.
This is a problem since the Korn constant generally depends on the underlying domain.
As pointed out in
Section 3
, the family of domains in only in
\mathrm{C}^{0,\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in(0,1)
, so standard approaches fall short here.
We note that this is another place where the 2D and 3D cases differ.
However, Lengeler showed in
[
34
, Proposition 2.9]
a Korn-type inequality for non-Lipschitz domains, where the Korn constant only depends on the bound of the structure displacement.
The lower regularity of the domain merely yields the boundedness for lower integrability parameters though.
We shall make these considerations precise in the sequel.
There are some further preparations required for this.
First, similarly as in (
3.4
), for
f
denoting a function defined on the fixed domain, and recalling the ALE mapping from
Subsection 3.1
, we find that

(6.6)

\nabla f=(\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}f)[\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}].

Moreover, note that

\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&(1+\frac{z}{R})\partial_{x}\omega_{N}^{n}\\
0&1&(1+\frac{z}{R})\partial_{y}\omega_{N}^{n}\\
0&0&1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}\end{pmatrix}.

From (
6.3
), it follows that for given
T>0
, the family
\omega_{N}^{n}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)
.
Thus, thanks to the embedding
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{W}^{1,s}(\Gamma)
for all
s<\infty
, we find that this family is also uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{W}^{1,s}(\Gamma)
.
Together with Assumption 1.B, this results in

(6.7)

\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{s}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})}\leq C\bigl(\|\omega_{N}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,s}(\Gamma)}+\|\omega_{N}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Gamma)}+1\bigr)\leq C,

where the (generic) constant
C>0
is independent of
n
and
N
.
Let now
p\in(1,2)
be arbitrary.
We then find
r\in(p,2)
and
r^{\prime}\in(2,\infty)
such that
\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{r^{\prime}}}=\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}
.
Making use of (
6.6
), Hölder's inequality together with (
6.7
), Assumption 1.B, which also yields that
\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}=1+\frac{\omega_{N}^{n}}{R}
is bounded from below, and
[
34
, Proposition 2.9]
, and employing the notation
\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}^{n+1}=\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\circ(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}})^{-1}
, we find that

(6.8)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right)^{\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\Bigl|\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}u_{N}^{n+1}\bigl[\nabla\Phi_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigr]\Bigr|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right)^{\frac{2}{p}}\leq C\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\bigl|\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right)^{\frac{2}{r}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigl|\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right)^{\frac{2}{r}}=C\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(t)}\bigl|\nabla\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right)^{\frac{2}{r}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{D}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}^{n+1})\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(t))}^{2}+\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}(t))}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=C\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigl|\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right).

From (
6.8
) and the observation that
[
34
, Proposition 2.9]
yields that the Korn inequality only depends on bounds of
\omega_{N}^{n}
in
\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)
and
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Gamma)
, which are in turn uniform in
n
and
N
, it now follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})}^{2}=(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right)^{\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left(\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigl|\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right)

for
C>0
independent of
n
and
N
.
As a result of (
6.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}\bigl|\boldsymbol{D}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1})\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq E_{0}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{\Omega_{\mathrm{f}}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega_{N}^{n}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq(\Delta t)\cdot N\cdot E_{0}=TE_{0}.

In total, we find that

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(1+T)E_{0},

where
C>0
is independent of
n
and
N
.
As we have already verified that the sequence
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3})
for all
p<2
, we infer that
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}
is indeed also uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{1,p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3})
for all
p<2
.

Next, we address the uniform boundedness of the Biot displacement
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
, for arbitrary
t\in(0,T)
.
As above, we argue that there is
n\in\{0,1,\dots,N-1\}
with
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}(t)=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}
.
Thus, using Poincaré's inequality, Korn's inequality for the Biot domain as asserted in
Subsection 5.2
and (
6.3
) together with the form of
E_{N}^{n+1}
as made precise in
Subsection 5.3
, we infer that

\|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}}^{2}\leq C\cdot\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}^{2}\leq C\cdot\|\boldsymbol{D}(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n+1})\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}^{2}\leq CE_{0},

where the constant
C>0
is again uniform in
N
.

Analogously as in (
6.5
), upon invoking the form of
E_{N}^{n+1}
, we deduce from (
6.3
) the uniform boundedness of
p_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)})
.
The same is valid for the uniform boundedness of
\omega_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma))
.
For the uniform boundedness of the pore pressure
p_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)})
, inequality (
6.3
) yields

\displaystyle\kappa(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{{\Omega}\mathcal{J}_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}\bigl|\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq E_{0}.}

To control the gradient of
p_{N}
rather than the transformed gradient, we start by invoking the identity
\nabla p_{N}^{n+1}=\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\cdot(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta})
.
In conjunction with Assumption 2.B, it then follows that

(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C}

for some
C>0
independent of
N
.
With regard to the identity
\nabla p_{N}^{n+1}=\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\cdot(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta})
as well as Assumption 2.C, which in turn implies that
|\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}|\leq c_{2}
, we then find that

(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\nabla p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq c_{2}^{2}\cdot(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\nabla_{^{(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n})^{\delta}}}p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C}}

for
C>0
independent of
N
.
Similarly as above for the fluid velocity, for
C
as just described, this eventually yields the uniform boundedness of the pore pressure
p_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)})
thanks to

\|p_{N}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)})}^{2}\leq C(\Delta t)\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{{\Omega}\bigl|\nabla p_{N}^{n+1}\bigr|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C.}

Concerning the remaining uniform boundedness assertions on the uniform boundedness of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}
as well as
\overline{\omega}_{N}
, we start by recalling
\partial_{t}\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}(t)=\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}
and
\partial_{t}\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)=\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}=\zeta_{N}(t)
for some
n\in\{0,1,\dots,N-1\}
.
Moreover, we observe that the uniform boundedness of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
follows in a similar manner as the above uniform boundedness of
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
.
On the other hand, for
t\in(0,T)
, invoking the above relation, and exploiting (
6.3
) along with the form of
E_{N}^{n+1}
, we find that

\|\partial_{t}\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}^{2}=\|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}^{2}\leq CE_{0},

where
C>0
is independent of
N
.
The uniform boundedness of
\overline{\omega}_{N}
in
\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma))
can be deduced similarly by virtue of (
6.2
) and the form of
E_{N}^{n+1}
.
∎

The corollary below is an immediate consequence of the uniform boundedness established in
Section 6
.

Corollary 6.4
.

Suppose that the assumptions stated in
Section 6
are satisfied.
Then there is a subsequence such that the following weak convergence results hold:

•

Fluid velocity:

–

\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{u}
weakly
∗
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3})
and

–

\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\tau_{\Delta t}\omega_{N}}\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{G}
weakly in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{p}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3\times 3})
for all
p\in(1,2)
, for some
\boldsymbol{G}
, where
\tau_{\Delta t}
denotes the time shift by
\Delta t
, i.e.,
\tau_{\Delta t}f(t,\cdot)=f(t-\Delta t,\cdot)
for a function
f
defined on
(0,T)
.

•

Biot displacement:

–

\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\eta}
weakly
∗
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
and

–

\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}\rightharpoonup\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
weakly
∗
in
\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
.

•

Biot pressure:

–

p_{N}\rightharpoonup p
weakly
∗
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)})
and

–

p_{N}\rightharpoonup p
weakly in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)})
.

•

Plate displacement:

–

\omega_{N}
satisfies
\omega_{N}\rightharpoonup\omega
weakly
∗
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma))
and

–

\overline{\omega}_{N}\rightharpoonup\overline{\omega}
weakly
∗
in
\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma))
.

Additionally, we have
\boldsymbol{\eta}=\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
and
\omega=\overline{\omega}
.

In
Subsection 8.1
, we will prove that
\boldsymbol{G}=\nabla_{\mathrm{f}}^{\omega}\boldsymbol{u}
.

Next, we show that the assumptions from
Section 6
are indeed fulfilled for sufficiently small
T>0
, and under suitable conditions on
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume that the initial data satisfy
|\omega_{0}|\leq R_{0}<R
, and that
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}
is such that
\Id+(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}
is invertible with
\det(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta})\geq c_{0}>0
on
\Omega
.
Then there exists
T>0
sufficiently small such that for all
N
, Assumptions 1.B, 2.B and 2.C from
Section 6
are satisfied.

Proof.

The proof of this result is different from the proof of the corresponding result in 2D due to different embedding properties.

First observe that it is clear that
\omega_{0}
is bounded, while the boundedness of
(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}
on
\Gamma
is a consequence of the invertibility of
\Id+(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}
and
\det(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta})\geq c_{0}>0
.
In particular, thanks the continuity of
|\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}|
and
|(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta})^{-1}|
on the compact set
\overline{\Omega}
, we find constants
\alpha_{1}
,
\alpha_{2}>0
such that
|\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}|\leq\alpha_{1}
and
|(\boldsymbol{I}+\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta})^{-1}|\leq\alpha_{2}
.

In the remainder of the proof, we show that there is a sufficiently small time
T>0
so that Assumptions 1.B, 2.B and 2.C from
Section 6
hold uniformly for all
N
and
n\Delta t
up to time
T
.
For this purpose, we first invoke the energy equality from
Subsection 5.3
.
Similarly as in (
6.3
), we find that

E_{N}^{k+\frac{1}{2}}\leq E_{0}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0ptE_{N}^{k+1}\leq E_{0}\kern 5.0pt\text{for all}\kern 5.0ptk=0,1,\dots,N-1.

Thus, after solving the two subproblems as exposed in
Subsection 5.1
and
Subsection 5.2
on the time step
[k\Delta t,(k+1)\Delta t]
, for a constant
C>0
merely depending on the initial energy
E_{0}
, we get

(6.9)

\displaystyle\|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\text{for}\kern 5.0ptn=0,1,\dots,k+1,

\displaystyle\|\omega_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\|_{\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\text{for}\kern 5.0ptn=0,1,\dots,k,\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{N}^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\text{for}\kern 5.0pt0\leq n+\frac{i}{2}\leq k+1\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pti=0,1.

The first step is to establish the uniform bound of the plate displacements
\omega_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}
.
To this end, we recall that
\partial_{t}\overline{\omega}_{N}=\zeta_{N}
, so the piecewise linear and piecewise constant character of
\overline{\omega}_{N}
and
\zeta_{N}
, respectively, joint with (
6.9
)
2
and (
6.9
)
3
yields that there is
C=C(E_{0})>0
independent of
N
such that

(6.10)

\displaystyle\|\overline{\omega}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,(k+1)\Delta t;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma))}

\displaystyle\leq C\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt\|\overline{\omega}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,(k+1)\Delta t;\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C.

Note that (
6.10
) means that
\overline{\omega}_{N}
is Lipschitz-continuous in time with values in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)
.
Next, we invoke that for
s\in(1,2)
, by complex interpolation, see e.g.,
[
37
, Section 2.1]
, we have
\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma)=[\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma),\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Gamma)]_{\frac{s}{2}}
.
Consequently, for all
t
and
t+\tau\in[0,(k+1)\Delta t]
with
\tau>0
, we obtain

\|\overline{\omega}_{N}(t+\tau)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma)}\leq C\cdot\|\overline{\omega}_{N}(t+\tau)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{1-\frac{s}{2}}\cdot\|\overline{\omega}_{N}(t+\tau)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{\frac{s}{2}}.

Combining the latter inequality with (
6.10
) and the above remark on the Lipschitz continuity in time, we infer that

(6.11)

\|\overline{\omega}_{N}(t+\tau)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma)}\leq C\cdot\tau^{1-\frac{s}{2}},

where
C=C(E_{0})>0
is again independent of
k
and
N
.
The choice
t=0
and
\tau=(k+1)\Delta t
joint with the embedding
\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{C}(\Gamma)
thanks to
s>1
then leads to

\|\omega_{N}^{k+1}-\omega_{0}\|_{\mathrm{C}(\Gamma)}\leq C\cdot[(k+1)\Delta t]^{1-\frac{s}{2}}\leq C\cdot T^{1-\frac{s}{2}}.

As the above constant
C>0
only depends on
E_{0}
, with regard to
|\omega_{0}|<R
, we may choose
T>0
sufficiently small such that
C\cdot T^{1-\frac{s}{2}}<R-\|\omega_{0}\|_{\mathrm{C}(\Gamma)}
, showing that the first part of Assumption 1.B can be guaranteed for
T>0
sufficiently small.

What is left to show is the boundedness of the trace of
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}
.
For this purpose, we employ the triangle inequality, the definition of
\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{n-1}}{\Delta t}
as well as (
6.9
)
1
to get

\|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}\leq(\Delta t)\sum_{n=1}^{k+1}\|\dot{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}\leq C(k+1)(\Delta t)\leq CT,

with
C=C(E_{0})>0
independent of
N
.
On the other hand, the triangle inequality, the construction of the plate subproblem in
Subsection 5.1
and (
6.9
)
3
imply that

\|\omega_{N}^{k+1}-\omega_{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq(\Delta t)\sum_{n=1}^{k+1}\|\zeta_{N}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C(\Delta t)(k+1)\leq CT.

Concatenating the previous two estimates, and invoking the odd extension from (
4.1
), we conclude that

\|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}_{)}}\leq C\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}}+\|\omega_{N}^{k+1}-\omega_{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\bigr)\leq CT.

Again, let us emphasize that
C=C(E_{0})>0
is independent of
N
.
Thanks to the regularization, for
C=C(\delta,E_{0})>0
, we find that

\|(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{k+1})^{\delta}-(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{3}(\Omega_{)}}\leq C\cdot T.

Since the trace is continuous from
\mathrm{H}^{3}(\Omega_{)}
to
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Gamma)
, and thanks to
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{C}(\Gamma)
and
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\Omega_{)}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{C}(\Omega_{)}
, for
C=C(E_{0},\delta)>0
independent of
N
, we deduce from the last estimate that

(6.12)

\left\|\left.(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{k+1})^{\delta}\right|_{\Gamma}-\left.(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}\right|_{\Gamma}\right\|_{\mathrm{C}(\Gamma)}\leq C\cdot T\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0pt\left\|\nabla(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}^{k+1})^{\delta}-(\boldsymbol{\eta}_{0})^{\delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{C}(\Gamma)}\leq C\cdot T.

At this stage, let us observe that
\det(\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{A})
depends continuously on
\boldsymbol{A}
, and the matrix norms
|\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{A}|
and
|(\boldsymbol{I}+\boldsymbol{A})^{-1}|
are continuous functions of
\boldsymbol{A}
.
Moreover, the constant
C
in (
6.12
) merely depends on
E_{0}
and
\delta
and, in particular, it is independent of
k
and
N
(although it may depend on
\delta
).
By virtue of (
6.12
), we may choose
T>0
sufficiently small such that the second part of Assumption 1.B and Assumptions 2.B and 2.C are satisfied, completing the proof.
∎

7.
Approximate solutions: Strong convergence via compactness arguments

The weak and weak
∗
convergences established in
Section 6
are insufficient to pass to the limit in the nonlinear terms in the semidiscrete formulation (
5.4
) and (
5.5
).
Instead, we need to upgrade to strong convergence results.
To that end, we apply the classical Aubin–Lions compactness theorem on fixed domains, together with the generalized Aubin–Lions framework of
[
45
]
. We first establish compactness for the plate displacement and for the displacement in the Biot domain, and then address the compactness of the fluid velocity on the moving fluid domain.

Throughout this section, we consider
T>0
sufficiently small, determined in
Section 6
.

7.1.
Compactness for the Biot displacement

In this subsection, we verify the strong convergence of the Biot structure displacement
\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}
by employing the classical Aubin–Lions compactness result.

Lemma 7.1
.

We have that
\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}^{3})\Subset\mathrm{C}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
.
In particular, we deduce the existence of a subsequence, still denoted by
(\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N})
, such that
\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{\eta}
strongly in
\mathrm{C}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
.

Proof.

For the compactness of the embedding, we first note that
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}\Subset\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}
by the Rellich–Kondrachov theorem.
Thus, the compactness of the embedding is a result of the classical Aubin–Lions compactness theorem.
For the existence of a convergent subsequence
(\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N})
, we still require the uniform boundedness of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}
in
\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3})\cap\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
.
The first part directly follows from
Section 6
.
In the same lemma, we have also shown that the Biot displacement
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{N}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{)}^{3})
.
The uniform boundedness of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{N}
can be concluded in the same way.
∎

7.2.
Compactness for the plate displacement

Next, we address the compactness for the plate displacement.
Here we observe that we can only deduce the strong convergence in
\mathrm{C}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
for
s<2
, owing to the use of the Arzela–Ascoli theorem and the required compactness of the Sobolev embedding, even though the plate displacements are uniformly boundedness in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma))
by
Section 6
.
The associated result is given below.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
s\in(0,2)
.
Then there exist subsequences
\overline{\omega}_{N}
and
\omega_{N}
such that
\overline{\omega}_{N}\to\omega
strongly in
\mathrm{C}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
and
\omega_{N}\to\omega
strongly in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
.

Proof.

Let us observe that for
s\in(0,2)
, there exists
s^{\prime}\in(0,2)
such that
s^{\prime}=s+\varepsilon
, where
\varepsilon>0
is sufficiently small.
From (
6.11
), for
\tau>0
as well as
t
,
t+\tau\in[0,T]
, we recall that

\|\overline{\omega}_{N}(t+\tau)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}\leq C\tau^{1-\frac{s^{\prime}}{2}},

where we emphasize that the constant
C
is independent of
N
.
This estimate establishes the equicontinuity of
(\overline{\omega}_{N})
.
At the same time, note that
\mathrm{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Gamma)\Subset\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma)
by the choice of
s^{\prime}=s+\varepsilon
.
On the other hand, the uniform boundedness of
\overline{\omega}_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
for
s\in(0,2)
is especially implied by
Section 6
upon noting that the proof is analogous to the one of the uniform boundedness of
\omega_{N}
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}_{0}^{2}(\Gamma))
.
Therefore, the Arzela–Ascoli theorem yields the existence of a subsequence, still denoted by
\overline{\omega}_{N}
such that
\overline{\omega}_{N}\to\omega
strongly in
\mathrm{C}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
.

For the second part of the assertion, the aim is to show that
\|\omega_{N}(t)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))}\to 0
.
To this end, observe that
\overline{\omega}_{N}(n\Delta t)=\omega_{N}(t)
for
n\Delta t\leq t<(n+1)\Delta t
.
Thus, additionally exploiting (
6.11
), we find the existence of
\tau\leq\Delta t
such that

\|\omega_{N}(t)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))}=\|\overline{\omega}_{N}(t+\tau)-\overline{\omega}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))}\leq C(\Delta t)^{1-\frac{s}{2}}\to 0\kern 5.0pt\text{as}\kern 5.0ptN\to\infty,

where we invoke again the independence of the constant
C
of
N
.
This shows that
\omega_{N}
and
\overline{\omega}_{N}
have the same limit in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
for
s\in(0,2)
and thus completes the proof.
∎

Before proceeding with the compactness for the Biot velocity, the plate velocity, the pore pressure and finally also the fluid velocity, we remark that in the test space
\mathcal{Q}_{N}^{n+1}
from (
5.3
), the pore pressure and the fluid velocity are decoupled from the Biot and plate velocity, so we may address the compactness arguments for these quantities separately.
In fact, we will decouple the test space into smaller test spaces for the Biot and place displacement and velocity, the pore pressure and the fluid velocity.
Due to the kinematic coupling condition at the plate interface, we must handle the Biot and plate velocity together, which is presented next.

7.3.
Compactness for the Biot and plate velocities

The aim of this subsection is to establish compactness results for the Biot and plate velocities
(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\zeta_{N})
.
More precisely, we intend to show the existence of convergent subsequences in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Omega_{)}^{3}\times\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
for
s\in(-\frac{1}{2},0)
.
The reasons for considering negative Sobolev spaces are twofold.
First, the main existence result of this work includes the case in which the Biot medium is purely elastic, namely, the viscoelasticity coefficients can be zero, which implies that the Biot velocities can only be
\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{)}^{3}
.
Secondly, in contrast with classical FSI problems with elastic structures and no-slip boundary conditions, where the interface velocity is the same as the trace of the fluid velocity, in the present construction of the splitting scheme for the regularized FPSI problem, the plate velocity
\zeta_{N}
generally does not coincide with the traces of the fluid velocity
\boldsymbol{u}_{N}\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{f}})^{3}
.
As a consequence, we cannot expect higher regularity of the plate velocities compared to the regularity resulting from the finite-energy space.
In other words, the plate velocities can only be expected to lie in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Gamma)
.

Below, we assert the compactness result for the Biot and plate velocities.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
s\in(-\frac{1}{2},0)
.
Then there is a subsequence, still denoted by
(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\zeta_{N})
, such that
(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{N},\zeta_{N})\to(\boldsymbol{\xi},\zeta)
strongly in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Omega_{)}^{3}\times\mathrm{H}^{s}(\Gamma))
.

Proof.
